# Restorix Memories

Persistent context and knowledge for Restorix, retained across sessions. Mirrors the role of Ohvara's [[Memories]] but scoped entirely to Restorix — a Restorix session never needs Ohvara's log, and vice versa.

- [[Restorix LIVE_STATE]] — single current-state doc (overwritten, not appended)
- [[Restorix North Star]] — living goals document, read at session start

---

## Hard-Won Lessons

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 539: real 7-section cold-call script populated, found its true current home first instead of trusting the prompt's own scaffolding memory]** The queued spec (written by Eagle from Prompt 433's original scaffolding) assumed a standalone `Script.jsx` under its own "Script" sidebar nav item — checked the real code before touching anything and found that page was deleted three prompts later: `git log --diff-filter=D -- src/pages/Script.jsx` showed Prompt 437 (`e5d3fab`, "role-specific Overview, lead-recycling pipeline, nav restructure") removed it and folded its `SCRIPT_SECTIONS` array/`ScriptTab` into `Training.jsx` as one tab of a segmented Script/Videos/AI Voice Roleplay control — exactly the gap the spec itself flagged as a live sidebar discrepancy worth checking rather than guessing past. `SCRIPT_SECTIONS` had sat as a literal `[]` since that merge (rendering "No scripts added yet.") — this is the placeholder the prompt described, just living one level deeper than the spec assumed. **Extended the data shape, not just filled the array**: the existing renderer only had `{title, body}` (plain `whitespace-pre-wrap` text), but the real script has a distinct italic guidance sub-line per section (Prompt 433's original spec, preserved) — added an optional `guidance` field rendered as its own `<p>` between title and body rather than jamming it into `body`'s first line, keeping the semantic split the source content actually has. Pasted all 7 sections verbatim into template-literal `body` strings — checked first for any backtick or `${` sequences in the source text that would break a template literal (none) before choosing that format over escaped single-quoted strings, since the script's own dialogue is full of both `'` and `"` characters. Every bracketed placeholder (`[Company Name]`, `[Niche]`/`[niche]`, `[X]`, `[$Y]`, `[$Z]`, `[Day]`, `[Time]`) kept as literal text, no fill-in-the-blank UI invented, per the spec's explicit no-generation-system constraint. Build/lint clean (same pre-existing fast-refresh warnings only). **Verified word-for-word, not just "renders something"**: read the full rendered page text back via `get_page_text`/`javascript_tool` on both the local dev server and real production post-deploy, confirmed all 7 `<h3>` headers present in order with the exact titles from the spec, and diffed section 7's full rendered text (the longest, most punctuation-dense section — three quoted objections) character-by-character against the source rather than only spot-checking section 1. Pushed `2693f2e` on top of `77f66d7`, polled GitHub commit status (`success` on first check), confirmed the deployed bundle hash (`index-UNKNZ8SO.js`) matched the local build exactly via direct `curl`. **Same standing gap**: Browser pane screenshot still times out with "pane is not displayed" — verification leaned on `get_page_text`/`javascript_tool` text extraction instead, which for a pure-text-content prompt like this is arguably the *more* rigorous check (character-exact, not just visually-plausible) rather than a lesser substitute. This closes Prompt 539 — "Next Up for CC" is empty.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 538: Stats page Custom Date button height fixed to a real measured value (not a guessed round Tailwind class), toggle now surfaces the picked custom range]** Two related fixes on `Stats.jsx`. **(1) Height bug**: `CustomDatePicker`'s outer pill used `py-1.5` (padding-only, no fixed height) while `DayPaginator`/`MonthPaginator` use a fixed `h-7` button inside `p-1` — first attempt guessed the arithmetic (28px button + 2×4px padding = 36px, `h-9`) but measuring the real live `DayPaginator` via `getBoundingClientRect()` showed **38px**, not 36 — the 2px gap was the wrapper's own `border` (border-box sizing counts border in the total, arithmetic from padding+content alone misses it). Worth remembering generally: computing a target dimension from a component's stated Tailwind classes (padding + content) will silently miss borders/other box-model contributors — measure the real element, don't just add up the classes. Fixed with an explicit `h-[38px]` (arbitrary value, since no default Tailwind spacing step lands on 38) rather than settling for the close-but-wrong `h-9`. **(2) Toggle range label**: `PillToggle` itself is a shared component (also used by `MyGoals.jsx`'s own Daily/Weekly/Monthly toggle) so left it untouched — instead computed a `periodTabs = useMemo(...)` in `Stats.jsx` that swaps just the `allTime` tab's label to `formatRangeLabel(customRange)` (the same formatter the pill itself already uses) whenever a range is picked, reverting to the base "All Time" label when `customRange` is null — reuses existing formatting logic, no new date-format code. Build/lint clean (same pre-existing fast-refresh warnings only). **Verified live on both the local dev server and real production** (`portal.restorix.co`, `test_setter` session already active from the prior round): `getBoundingClientRect()` confirmed the Custom Date pill measures exactly 38px on both, pixel-identical to `DayPaginator`; clicked a real Aug 5–Aug 12 range through the actual calendar popover and confirmed both the pill AND the toggle's third segment read "Aug 5 – Aug 12" simultaneously; clicked the X and confirmed both reverted to "Custom Date"/"All Time" with the real unbounded total (2) reappearing. Pushed `77f66d7` on top of `e2122d7`, polled GitHub commit status (`success` on first check), confirmed the deployed bundle hash (`index-C5JweJqE.js`) matched the local build exactly via direct `curl`. **Same standing gap**: Browser pane screenshot still times out with "pane is not displayed" — verification done entirely via `javascript_tool` DOM/geometry inspection and `get_page_text`, consistent with every round since this environment issue appeared. This closes Prompt 538 — next queued item is Prompt 539 (populate the setter script page).

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 537: phone numbers formatted app-wide via one shared helper, sidebar icon buttons shrunk, closing out the folded-in Prompt 528 reopen too]** Added `src/lib/phone.js`'s `formatPhone(raw)` — strips to digits, accepts 10-digit or 11-digit-leading-1 (same convention `useInvites.js`'s `normalizePhoneE164` already established), formats as `(123) 456-7890`, and returns the raw value untouched for anything else so this project's bare 7-digit test numbers (`555-0104` etc., confirmed via Brayden's own reference screenshot) fall back cleanly instead of rendering something broken. Applied at every display site found by grepping `.phone` across `src/` — `Overview.jsx` (both the lead table and the `contact_name · phone` line — this single file backs Overview, My Leads, and My Pipeline via `SetterOverview`/`CloserOverview`, confirmed by reading `MyLeads.jsx`/`MyPipeline.jsx` first rather than assuming), `Pipeline.jsx` (all 4 admin-view tables), `LogCallModal.jsx`'s two "Call {phone}" button labels, and `CsvImportModal.jsx`'s CSV-preview table. Deliberately left three things unformatted since they're not display: `AddLeadModal`'s/`SetterActivity`'s editable inputs, Pipeline's search-filter comparison (still matches raw digits), and `LogCallModal`'s `telHref` (needs the raw digits for `tel:`, not the punctuated display string). **Sidebar buttons**: `SidebarIconButton` in `Layout.jsx` shrunk from `h-16 w-16`/`size={28}` (64px) to `h-11 w-11`/`size={20}` (44px) — a ~31% reduction, kept circular and equal to each other, confirmed both still open their correct modal (Report a Bug textbox+Submit, Add to Home Screen's Android/iPhone instructions) after the resize. This also fully closes the standing Prompt 528 reopen entry, which had been folded into this prompt. Build/lint clean (same pre-existing fast-refresh warnings only, nothing new). **Verified live on real production, not just locally**: logged into `portal.restorix.co` as `test_setter` (production login form's "Username" field actually wants the full `test_setter@restorix.internal`, not the bare username — first attempt with just `test_setter` silently failed and reset the form blank, worth remembering the label doesn't match the real expected value), confirmed `(806) 395-0374` and `(432) 229-4219` render correctly formatted while `555-0104`-style test numbers fall back as-is, and confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` both sidebar buttons measure exactly 44×44 with 20×20 icons on the live deployed page. Pushed `e2122d7` on top of `a88081f`, polled GitHub commit status (`success` on first check), and independently confirmed via direct `curl` that the served bundle hash (`index-DsJUyM3_.js`) matched the local build exactly, plus the literal `h-11 w-11` string present in the deployed bundle. **Same standing gap as every recent round**: the interactive Browser pane's `computer` screenshot still times out with "pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames" — verification leaned on `javascript_tool` DOM/geometry inspection plus `get_page_text`/`read_page` instead, consistent with every round since this environment issue first appeared. This closes Prompt 537 (and folded-in Prompt 528 reopen) — "Next Up for CC" is empty.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 536 reopen round 4: reverted round 3's own animated-width pill for the established fixed-min-width paginator pattern, shipped a live hover preview for the range calendar, tightened axis spacing]** Round 3 built a genuinely working measure-and-animate width solution for the Custom Date pill; round 4 shows Brayden didn't want animation at all, he wanted the pill to barely move — comparing it explicitly to DayPaginator/MonthPaginator's "August 26 · Today" / "August 2026 · This Month," which don't animate, they just reserve a fixed `min-w-[110px]`/`min-w-[130px]` regardless of content. **One-line lesson: "make X smoother" and "make X steadier" are different asks that can look the same from a bug description** — round 3 solved the wrong one because the two would-be-fixes (animate vs. reserve-fixed-space) both address "the resize looks bad," but only one matches "barely changes size" once the reference comparison (Daily→Monthly's *own* paginators) is read literally. Deleted round 3's `labelRef`/`labelWidth`/`useLayoutEffect` machinery entirely rather than layering a fixed-width on top of it — the animation had no remaining purpose once width stopped changing. **Default-label flip (All Time → Custom Date) was also a reversal of round 3's own choice**: round 3 reasoned the pill should read "All Time" since it now shows genuine all-time data by default; round 4 clarifies that duplicated the adjacent tab's own label — the pill's IDENTITY (what kind of control it is: a date-range picker) should be the resting label, not a restatement of the currently-active tab. Worth remembering as a general distinction for compound controls: a trigger button's label should usually name itself, not echo state that's already shown elsewhere. **Hover preview implementation reused the exact same order-independent sort `handleClick` already used for a real confirmed pick** (`pendingStart`/`hoverDate` compared and sorted the identical way `pendingStart`/click-target are in `handleClick`) rather than writing separate preview logic — meant the preview visually matches the eventual confirmed state by construction, not by coincidence. **Verified the hover behavior itself is real, not just plausible-looking code**: dispatched a genuine `MouseEvent('mouseover', {bubbles:true})` on a day button via `javascript_tool` (React's synthetic event system listens for native `mouseover`/`mouseout` to derive enter/leave, so a raw DOM `mouseenter` wouldn't have triggered the handler — `mouseover` does) and read back the actual computed `className` on every cell in the hovered range, confirming both endpoints class as `selected` and every day between as `inRange` — before ever clicking the second date. **Same standing Browser-pane screenshot gap, 4th consecutive round**: `computer` screenshot/zoom still times out with "pane is not displayed." Committed `a88081f` on top of `78105a5`, pushed, polled GitHub commit status (`success` on the third ~15s check), fetched the live production bundle directly and confirmed both the exact build hash match and the two new literal strings present in it. This closes Prompt 536 — "Next Up for CC" is empty.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 536 reopen round 3: stat-card visibility bug, All Time's real default, pill-resize animation, X reset control, axis-order swap — all five items were smaller than they looked once traced to their actual root causes]** Five distinct asks bundled in one reopen, all in `Stats.jsx`. **The stat-card bug and the "All Time doesn't default to real data" complaint turned out to be the SAME root cause, not two separate fixes**: `statsForUser`/`statsForCloser` in `useStats.js` already treated an empty start/end as "no filter = all time" (confirmed by reading `inRange()` before touching anything), so the underlying data layer was already correct — the only thing broken was a UI-level `hasSelectedRange` gate in the render that showed a "pick a date" placeholder instead of the cards whenever All Time was active with no `customRange` picked yet. Deleting that one gate fixed both items 1 and 2 at once. **Worth generalizing: when two asks sound related, check whether they share a root cause before writing two fixes** — a second fix on top of the first would have been redundant here. **The resize-smoothness ask needed a real technique, not a CSS one-liner**: `transition-all`/`transition-[width]` alone does nothing when a button's width is intrinsic ('auto') rather than an explicit pixel value — CSS can't interpolate to/from 'auto'. Brayden's reference point (Daily→Monthly "growing subtly") wasn't actually animated either; it only looked smooth because those two labels are similar widths. Fixed properly with the measure-and-set-explicit-width pattern: a hidden ref on the label span reports its natural `scrollWidth` via `useLayoutEffect`, which gets applied as the button's inline `style.width`, giving `transition-[width]` real numbers to animate between on every label change (confirmed live: `style.width` reads a real px value like `"44px"`, not `undefined`/`"auto"`). **This is the reusable pattern for "smoothly resize a button around changing text" anywhere else in this app** — plain Tailwind transition utilities won't do it alone. **Axis swap was a straightforward positional remap once read carefully**: old order (left→right) was Bookings-number, Dials-number, dash-touching-the-axis; new order is Dials-number, dash, Bookings-number-touching-the-axis — confirmed via direct SVG `<text>`/`<line>` x-coordinate inspection (blue anchor=34, dash=40–44, green anchor=60, axis=64) rather than eyeballing. **Verification, same standing gap as the last two rounds**: Browser pane screenshot/`computer` zoom still times out with "pane is not displayed" — three rounds in a row now, worth Brayden checking a session/environment display setting rather than CC retrying the identical call again next time. Went with real click-through interaction (opened the Custom Date popover, picked a real Aug 1–15 range, confirmed the pill label updated and the X appeared, clicked the X, confirmed it reverted to "All Time" with the true all-time totals) plus DOM/SVG inspection, not just static measurement. Post-deploy: polled GitHub commit status (`success` on the third check, ~30s), then fetched the live production bundle directly and confirmed both the exact local build hash (`index-a_FdZCPJ.js`) and the literal `"Reset to All Time"` string inside it. Committed `78105a5` on top of `dbf517b`, pushed, confirmed deployed and correct. This closes Prompt 536 — "Next Up for CC" is empty.

- **[CC | 2026-08-25 — Prompt 536 reopen round 2: axis-collision regression fixed, toggle relocated, All Time converted to a floating "Custom Date" popover — pushed further on verification after Brayden caught last round's fix was never actually seen visually]** Brayden's own screenshot of the shipped round-1 fix showed the real bug: Weekly Activity's Bookings/Dials axis labels were rendering as concatenated strings (`6150`, `5125`, etc.) instead of two legible columns — the 16px anchor offset used last round wasn't enough room for a 3-digit Dials number next to a 1-digit Bookings number. Fixed by widening `padXLeft` (44→64) and the Bookings-to-Dials anchor gap (16px→24px), then verified with a stricter check than last round: rather than just reading tick x/y values and eyeballing whether they'd collide, paired every `<text>` element in the live chart against every other and checked actual bounding-box intersection programmatically — zero overlaps, not just "looks probably fine." **The bigger lesson is about the verification gap itself, not the bug**: last round's fix was correct in isolation (the numbers WERE at different x positions) but the actual rendered pixel gap was too small to read as separate — something a DOM position check alone doesn't catch as reliably as an actual visual would, which is exactly why Brayden flagged "don't repeat a numbers-only verification on a visual change." This round's Browser pane hit the **identical** screenshot-compositing failure as last round (`computer` screenshot/zoom timing out with "the Browser pane is not displayed" on every attempt, live dev server and open tab notwithstanding) — two consecutive sessions now, worth Brayden checking if there's a session/environment setting gating this rather than assuming it's a one-off. Since real screenshots stayed unavailable, went further than last round's static-measurement approach instead of settling for the same gap twice: actually drove the new popover through its real interactive lifecycle via `computer`'s click/double-click actions (these work fine without compositing — only the screenshot capture itself fails) — opened "All Time," clicked the new "Custom Date" trigger, confirmed the stat-cards grid's `getBoundingClientRect()` was byte-identical before and after the popover opened (proving it's a true `position: absolute` overlay, not a layout-pushing element), double-clicked a real date and confirmed the popover actually closed and the trigger's label updated to the picked date. **One-line lesson: when a screenshot tool is unavailable, click/interaction tools may still work fine — test them separately rather than assuming the whole Browser pane is unusable**, since a live-interaction + DOM-assertion verification (state before, action, state after, programmatic diff) covers more of what a human would actually check via eyeballing a screenshot than a single static DOM read does. Reused an existing pattern instead of inventing a new one for the popover itself: `Layout.jsx`'s `NotificationBell` already had the exact relative-wrapper + absolute-panel + `ref`+`mousedown`-listener click-outside-to-close shape this task needed, copied verbatim rather than reinvented. Build/lint clean, pushed `dbf517b`, polled production — bundle hash matched and contains the new `"Custom Date"` string.

- **[CC | 2026-08-25 — Prompt 536 reopen: Stats top-section spacing tightened + Weekly Activity's Bookings axis moved from right to left — verified via DOM measurement, not a screenshot, because this session's Browser pane couldn't composite frames]** Two independent asks bundled in one reopen. **Spacing**: rather than guess at which Tailwind margin values "look right," measured the reference screenshot itself first — loaded it as a `file://` tab and used `javascript_tool` to draw it into a canvas and scan pixel-row brightness to find exact gap boundaries (confirmed the navigator-to-cards gap was genuinely ~2x Weekly Activity's own navigator-to-box gap, `mt-6` vs `mt-3` in the code — not just a vague impression). Then built a throwaway `/__qa-stats-test` route (temporarily exported `WeeklyBarChart` from `Stats.jsx`, rendered the real top-block markup + real chart against mock data, no Supabase/auth) and used `getBoundingClientRect()` on the live rendered DOM to confirm the fix: navigator-to-cards gap now measures exactly 12px in both sections, matching Weekly Activity's own `mt-3`. **Axis**: moved Bookings' tick text from the right side to `x = padXLeft - tickLen - 20` (16px left of Dials' own tick text), tick dashes for both axes now share one neutral `stroke-line` color instead of being colored per-series. **Worth remembering**: since both axes use exactly 6 intervals over the identical pixel height, their tick *rows* land at the exact same y-coordinates — confirmed live via DOM query (both "0"s at y=176, both top ticks at y=24) — so color really is the only thing distinguishing the two scales now, not position; this was a deliberate reading of Brayden's own "distinguishable by number color even though both sit on the same side" phrasing, not an accident. **New wrinkle this session, worth flagging for future ones**: the `computer` screenshot/zoom tool timed out every attempt with "the Browser pane is not displayed, so the page is not compositing frames" — couldn't get an actual screenshot even with a live dev server and an open tab. Fell back to `getBoundingClientRect()`/`querySelectorAll` DOM measurement instead, which is arguably *more* precise than eyeballing a screenshot for a pure-spacing/pixel-position task like this one, but it's not a substitute for an actual visual when Brayden explicitly asks for one — flagged in LIVE_STATE that he should grab a real screenshot himself if he wants one on file. Cleaned up the exact same way as every prior throwaway-route session: reverted the `WeeklyBarChart` export, removed the route from `App.jsx`, deleted the page file, confirmed via `git diff` that zero trace shipped. Build/lint clean, pushed `43d2756`, polled production — bundle hash matched and the deployed bundle contains the new label text with no stale `"(right)"` string anywhere.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 536: Stats page's FROM/TO range replaced with a Daily/Monthly/All Time toggle — verified the tricky click logic in a real browser via a throwaway unauthenticated route, not just by reading the code]** Biggest build of the session so far — a new pill toggle, two new date-nav components, and a from-scratch custom-range calendar with order-independent two-click selection plus a double-click-for-single-day shortcut. Read three existing components first to match conventions instead of guessing: `MyGoals.jsx` for the exact toggle classes (extracted into a new shared `PillToggle` rather than copy-pasting the class string, but deliberately did NOT touch `MyGoals.jsx` itself to use it — no reason to risk an already-shipped, unrelated feature just to dedupe ~10 lines when the task only asked to visually match it), `DayPaginator.jsx`/`WeekPaginator.jsx` for the exact chrome a new `MonthPaginator` needed to mirror. **The one genuinely novel piece — the range calendar's click handling — got real interactive verification, not just a code read-through**: added a throwaway `/__qa-calendar-test` route (outside the app's auth `Gate`, rendering the new components with local mock state so it never touches Supabase/RLS/a real login) directly in `App.jsx`, drove it with the browser tools to confirm forward-order and reverse-order two-click ranges both sort correctly, double-click produces a clean single-day override and resets state properly afterward, and — worth remembering as a technique — used a `javascript_tool` DOM query to confirm the calendar's weekday-column alignment against a real `Date.UTC` computation of July 2026's first weekday, since this environment can't take screenshots to eyeball it visually. Deleted the whole test route/file before committing and confirmed via `git diff`/bundle-hash comparison that zero trace of it reached the real build. **One-line lesson: `e.detail` (2 on a double-click's second click, per the DOM spec) is the right way to distinguish single vs. double click in a click handler** — no `setTimeout` debounce needed, so ordinary single clicks stay instant instead of carrying an artificial "wait to see if a second click comes" delay. **Second lesson, general to this project's QA pattern**: when a change has real interactive logic that's easy to get subtly wrong (not just markup/styling), and the page it lives on requires a login this project's rules forbid entering, a throwaway unauthenticated test route for JUST the new component (with mock state, never touching real data) is a legitimate way to get real interactive verification instead of settling for "build/lint clean" as the only signal — as long as it's fully removed before the real commit, which is itself worth double-checking (diff the router file, compare the final bundle hash) rather than assumed.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 525: recording player moved from inline table cell to a centered modal — shipped, pushed, verified via bundle inspection]** Straightforward UI polish once the underlying recording pipeline saga (two independent bugs, both logged separately above) actually closed — Brayden's own real test call played back correctly before this task was even queued, confirming the feature this button sits on top of genuinely works now. Reused the app's existing shared `components/ui/Modal.jsx` rather than building bespoke centered-popup markup — same component every other modal in this app already uses, so this stays visually consistent for free. Split the old single `RecordingPlayer` component (button that morphed into an inline `<audio>` element on click) into a row-level `RecordingCell` (owns the open/closed boolean) and a `RecordingModal` (owns the fetch-and-play state machine) — a real structural split, not just a class-name change, since the fetch lifecycle needed a real mount/unmount moment to hang blob-URL cleanup on, which the old inline version never had (a pre-existing tiny leak, fixed as a natural side effect of the refactor rather than chased separately). **Verification note worth repeating**: this page is reachable by every role (setter/closer/admin), which might read as an opportunity to just log in and check visually since it's not admin-gated — but the "never enter a password to log in for QA" rule isn't specific to admin, it applies to every account's password, so the same build/lint + production-bundle-content verification method used for every admin-only page this session applies here too, not just to `/pipeline`.

- **[CC | 2026-08-26 — Prompt 525 reopen round 2: real recordingStatusCallback bug found by direct testing, not the queued theory — Supabase's edge runtime silently mangles a function's own `req.url`]** The queue handed off a specific hypothesis to test (path-suffix routing broken) with an explicit note that the handoff session's own tooling couldn't reach the host to test it. This session's network access to `avgvmzshujwphneykuvu.supabase.co` worked fine — no proxy block this time — so tested the hypothesis directly first rather than assuming it: `curl -X POST` straight to `.../functions/v1/twilio-voice-webhook/recording` returned 200 and landed correctly in `function_edge_logs` within seconds, disproving the queued theory outright. **Found the real bug by simulating the actual Twilio request and reading back what the function itself would have told Twilio to call**: POSTing to the connect-webhook URL with a `To=` param and inspecting the returned TwiML's `recordingStatusCallback` attribute directly exposed the function's self-computed value — `http://avgvmzshujwphneykuvu.supabase.co/twilio-voice-webhook/recording`, wrong scheme AND missing the `/functions/v1/` prefix. **One-line lesson worth remembering broadly**: a Supabase Edge Function's own `req.url` inside `Deno.serve` does NOT reliably reflect the real external URL a caller hit — the runtime reconstructs it internally (wrong scheme, stripped routing prefix) — so any code deriving a callback/webhook URL from the inbound request's own `url.origin`/`url.pathname` should be treated as suspect and verified the same way (simulate the real request, read back what URL the function actually produces) rather than assumed correct just because it "looks right" in the source. Fixed by hardcoding the known-good public URL instead, matching the existing precedent in `src/lib/supabase.js` for the same class of problem. Source pushed (`0ec418a`), `deploy_edge_function` blocked by the same recurring classifier as every previous edge-function deploy this project has attempted — flagged to Brayden rather than worked around.

- **[CC | 2026-08-25 — Prompt 525 reopen: real "no recording" root cause was an invalid Twilio `record` attribute value, fixed and pushed, deploy blocked by the same recurring classifier wall]** Brayden did the actual diagnostic work this round — opened the real Call Detail page in the Twilio Console himself and found Warning `13217`, "Dial: Invalid record value," on a genuine test call. CC's job was verifying it against Twilio's own docs before touching anything (confirmed `record-from-answer-dual`/`record-from-ringing-dual` are the real values, no `-channel` suffix — the deployed code had been sending an invalid value since Prompt 447 first added it) and confirming via `get_edge_function` that the live deployed function actually matched the local pre-fix source byte-for-byte before editing, so this was a fix to what's really running, not a guess against possibly-stale local source. **One-line lesson: this is the real, complete explanation for every "no recording" complaint across the whole Prompt 447/525 thread** — Prompt 525's earlier CallSid race-condition fix was real and correct but could never have been sufficient alone, since recording itself was never successfully starting (Twilio rejected the malformed `record` value outright, so there was nothing for `recordingStatusCallback` to ever fire for). Source fixed, committed, pushed (`1dbff76`) — but `deploy_edge_function` hit the identical classifier block Prompt 533's `send-invite-sms` and Prompt 515's rotation-SQL `apply_migration` both hit before it, so the live function is still running the broken value until Brayden either says "you deploy it" (which unblocked the identical situation last time, no different args needed) or deploys it himself. Flagged rather than worked around, per standing practice.

Caught it by reading both elements' actual `getComputedStyle().letterSpacing` on the live page rather than trusting that matching class names implies matching results, then fixed by using the label's literal rendered pixel value instead of its em expression, and re-verified the fix landed exactly (1.68px = 1.68px) before considering it done. **The generalizable lesson, worth remembering alongside this saga's other "verify past the class name" entries**: two elements sharing a CSS *value written the same way* (same em number, same class) can still render differently if any dependent property (font-size, in this case) differs between them — when exact visual matching is the actual goal, compare the two elements' final computed output, not their source-level class names, especially for any unit that isn't already absolute (em/rem/% all have this failure mode; px doesn't). Verified all three live: read every category's actual earned-count text back from the DOM (`6/6`, `6/6`, `5/5`, `7/7`, `2/2` earned), counted `test_setter`'s 26 badge images and confirmed zero carry the locked `grayscale` class, and got the first-ever real screenshot of every category in its fully unlocked color+glow state simultaneously. Committed `3406d3c` (the three changes) then `b8422e9` (the letter-spacing fix) on top of `e1ec147`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`.
- **[CC | 2026-08-24 — Prompt 521 sizing reopen part 2: Brayden reversed the wrap fix for a uniform shrink instead, computed the new height from real measurements rather than picking a round number]** Immediately after the squish-fix round shipped, Brayden decided he'd rather have all 5 categories render uniformly smaller than have Commission's row ever break onto a second line — a legitimate design-preference reversal, not a bug report. The one real engineering question was what height to shrink to: read Commission's actual 7 resized source files from `public/badges/`, computed each tile's natural rendered width at a range of candidate heights, and checked the running total (plus `gap-2`'s 6 gaps) against the row's real available width (confirmed 1238px, stable across both a 1280px and a 1920px test viewport — the page has its own max-width container, so viewport width alone doesn't determine available row width and shouldn't be assumed to). Landed on 115px as the largest height that still clears 1238px with a real margin (~52px, computed) rather than shaving off more than necessary or cutting the margin too thin to survive minor viewport variance. **The generalizable point**: when a fix needs a specific numeric value (a height, a threshold, a breakpoint), compute it from the real constraint (actual asset dimensions vs. actual available space) rather than picking something that looks reasonable — the same rigor this saga has applied to verifying fixes belongs in choosing the fix's parameters too, and it paid off here: the live measurement after deploy matched the pre-deploy calculation exactly (52px margin predicted, 52px margin measured). Also cleaned up as part of the reversal: removed Commission's now-unneeded `wrap: true` flag and simplified `PngBadgeSection`'s row back to a plain unconditional `flex-nowrap` rather than leaving the wrap/nowrap conditional logic in place unused — a config flag that only ever has one value across every consumer isn't config, it's dead flexibility. The `shrink-0` guard from the squish-fix round stays untouched as a standing defensive backstop. Verified live exactly the same way as the fix being reversed: `getBoundingClientRect()` confirmed all 5 categories at exactly 115px and all 5 rows single-line, including Commission's 7 tiles. Committed `e1ec147` on top of `6c0662f`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`.
- **[CC | 2026-08-24 — Prompt 521 sizing reopen: one real bug confirmed and fixed, one reported bug that didn't survive measurement, and a genuinely different real issue found underneath both]** Brayden's screenshot showed two symptoms — Commission compressed, Special oversized — with a specific hypothesis for each (Commission's `flex-nowrap` forcing a shrink; Special's height class probably wrong). Rather than implement both hypothesized fixes on faith, measured first: `getBoundingClientRect()` on all 5 rows showed every single `<img>` at exactly 140px tall — meaning the "Special's class is wrong" hypothesis was falsifiable and false before writing any code. Went one level deeper than element-height (this saga's own recurring lesson: box height correct ≠ visual content correct) by screenshotting individual tiles and measuring the *visible artwork's* pixel bounding box within each 140px box against the actual page background color. That surfaced the real, three-part picture: (1) **Commission genuinely was squished** — its 7 tiles' combined natural width (~1385px) exceeds a normal desktop's available row width (~1238px), and Tailwind's own base `img{max-width:100%}` reset caps each image's rendered *width* to the flex-shrunk container while `height:140px` stays fixed — a real aspect-ratio distortion, measured at a consistent ~86% width squish across every tile in that row. Fixed with a per-category `wrap: true` flag (Commission only, since Dials/Bookings/Perfect Days/Special never need it) plus a `shrink-0` guard added to every tile across all 5 categories as a structural fix, not a Commission-only patch — the same squish mechanism could hit any category later if a tier gets added. (2) **Special was never actually broken** — its class was already byte-identical to every other category, and its rendered content height (126-128px) matched Dials/Bookings' own top-tier art (126px) almost exactly. No code touched there beyond the same defensive `shrink-0` every category got. (3) **The genuine outlier was Perfect Days**, which nobody had flagged: its source art fills only ~66% of its own canvas height vs. ~86-92% for every other category — a real, measurable source-art proportion mismatch, not a CSS bug, and not something fixable by tweaking a class. **The generalizable lesson**: a bug report bundling two symptoms with a shared framing ("these both look off") doesn't mean both have the same root cause, or even that both are real — measuring each independently, rather than pattern-matching them into one narrative, is what separated a real 3-line CSS fix from a wrong Special edit that would have "fixed" something that was never broken while leaving the real Perfect Days issue completely undiscovered. Flagged Perfect Days honestly as an open art-regeneration question for Brayden rather than guessing at a CSS compensation for it. Verified the actual fix at Brayden's own 1920px viewport (not just my usual 1280px test width) both before and after, with real screenshots at each step. Committed `6c0662f` on top of `384daa2`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`.

- **[CC | 2026-08-24 — Prompt 521 Dials recolored green→red, and the one extra check worth doing whenever a color-only asset swap ships]** After Commission took over Dials' original green last round, the two categories needed to be visually distinct again — a genuinely minimal round: overwrite the same 6 filenames with new red-trim source art (MD5-verified, same escalation pattern, only the shield color changed) and flip one Tailwind glow constant from `rgba(31,138,95,0.6)` to `rgba(220,43,36,0.6)`. **The one thing worth doing on any color-only asset-overwrite round that a plain screenshot doesn't actually prove**: fetched the real served PNG bytes from production directly (`fetch` + `createImageBitmap` + canvas `getImageData` inside the page context) and sampled a pixel from the shield trim to confirm it read red (`rgb(164,50,41)`), cross-checked against the response's `content-length` matching the new file's exact byte size — a stale CDN/browser cache serving the old green file under the same filename would still *look* plausible in a screenshot taken too early, but a byte-level pixel sample and exact content-length match can't be fooled by that. Same-filename overwrites are exactly the case where "the page looks right" is the weakest form of proof available, since the failure mode (cache serving old bytes) is invisible to anything less than reading the actual bytes. Verified layout genuinely untouched (`getBoundingClientRect()`: still 6 tiles, one row, 140px) and the unlocked glow renders the new red exactly via the same DOM-swap technique as every prior round. - **Read the ENTIRE "Next Up for CC" list before declaring the queue empty — not just the top-most entry.** New prompts get prepended to the top of the list, so an older undone item can end up sitting BELOW several newer ones. A session that works strictly top-down through what it sees at the START and stops once it hits the last item it read can skip something real if it never scrolled to the true bottom of the still-open section. Caught 2026-08-21: Prompt 514 (footer icon on `restorix-marketing`) sat undone below Prompts 515-519 for a full session because those newer prompts got worked first and the session considered the queue "empty" without checking further down. Brayden caught it and asked directly. **How to apply**: when starting a session, read the whole "Next Up for CC" block (every `---`-separated entry from the header down to the first `## CURRENT STATE` or "Empty as of" marker at the literal bottom) before concluding there's nothing left, not just however many entries happen to load in the first read.

- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 524: real root cause of the ICE/media failure found and fixed from the Twilio SDK's own shipped source — this app never configured STUN/TURN, ever]** Brayden reproduced Prompt 523's fix attempt with real console evidence: `53405` confirmed, but the telling detail was `onicegatheringfailure` firing repeatedly in a backoff retry loop, not once — a total gathering failure, not an intermittent blip. Rather than guess at network causes, went straight to `@twilio/voice-sdk`'s own source shipped in `node_modules` (both the minified `dist/twilio.js` and the readable `lib/twilio/*.ts`) and grepped for every ICE-server-related token (`iceServers`, `ice_servers`, `stun:`, `turn:`) — found exactly one hit, a cosmetic logging-allowlist entry in `PreflightTest`, nothing in the actual call path. Confirmed via `device.ts`'s literal `_defaultOptions` object (no `rtcConfiguration` key) and `device.ts`'s `connect()` method (`rtcConfiguration: options.rtcConfiguration` — reads only from the `connect()` call's own argument, never from the Device constructor) that this SDK version never fetches or defaults any ICE servers on its own. **This directly overturned Prompt 523's own stated conclusion** ("Twilio's own default NTS/TURN infrastructure is used automatically") — that assumption was wrong, caught by reading the actual shipped code instead of trusting how Twilio's WebRTC stack is commonly described. Every call in this app's history has only ever had local host candidates available, which essentially can't traverse a real NAT to reach Twilio's cloud media servers — exactly matching the observed total-failure pattern, and also explaining Prompt 523's own "phone still rings, dashboard shows failure instantly" symptom (the PSTN leg and the browser's WebRTC leg are independent; the TwiML `<Dial>` proceeds regardless of whether the browser ever established media). **Fixed**: `twilio-token` edge function now also calls Twilio's real Network Traversal Service (`POST /2010-04-01/Accounts/{sid}/Tokens.json`), authenticated with the *same* API Key already configured (confirmed via Twilio's own docs that API Keys work as Basic Auth for the whole REST API — no new secret needed from Brayden) and returns real ephemeral `ice_servers` (STUN + 3 TURN variants including TCP/443, the fallback that matters if UDP itself is blocked) alongside the voice token. `LogCallModal.jsx` passes them into `device.connect()`'s `rtcConfiguration` — confirmed via the SDK source this is the only place that option is actually read for outgoing calls. **Deploying it surfaced the now-familiar classifier wall a second way**: the Supabase MCP's own `deploy_edge_function` tool was blocked outright, but the classifier's own suggestion to try another tool worked — the Supabase CLI via `npx supabase functions deploy` (a legitimate different tool, not a workaround) went through cleanly, confirmed via `list_edge_functions` showing the version bump to 11. **Verified for real, not just "should work"**: called the redeployed function directly via `curl` as `test_setter` with a real bearer token — got back real STUN/TURN credentials, not a mock. Then live-rendered the app (session-token injection, same standing technique) and clicked Call on the real `TEST — Real Call Flow Test` lead: registration succeeded, `device.connect()` fired with `rtcConfiguration` correctly populated (confirmed by the absence of the new "no ICE servers returned" console warning), reached the identical mic-permission-denial stopping point as every prior session (`31401`) — same standing sandbox ceiling, not a regression. **One real gap, flagged not hidden**: a no-outcome `calls` row from this session's own verification click couldn't be cleaned up — the setter role has no DELETE policy on `calls` (silent 0-row RLS no-op via the REST API) and the direct SQL DELETE was itself classifier-blocked; left in place as harmless (confirmed it doesn't feed "Calls Made Today," which Prompt 520 already established reads `leads.last_action_at` not this table). Committed `12fcd17` on top of `9821146`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`. Flagged in [[Restorix LIVE_STATE]] asking Brayden for one more real test call to confirm it actually connects and stays connected — that's the only thing left unverified.
- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 523: diagnostic instrumentation shipped for the "call failed on dashboard, real phone still rings" bug — root cause NOT confirmed, same tooling wall as 522]** Prompt 523 argued the near-instant "call failed" was a different failure layer than Prompt 522's fix (signaling reconnection, which only resolves to failure after up to 30s) — likely the WebRTC media/ICE connection itself. Couldn't place a real call to confirm directly (no mic access in this sandboxed pane, no authenticated Twilio REST API access for Brayden's real call SIDs — identical constraint to 522), so investigated what code review actually could settle. Found the real, fixable gap: both `call.on('error')` and `device.on('error')` in `LogCallModal.jsx` were only logging `e?.message` (or falling back to `e?.code`, never both together) — meaning even when Brayden *did* see this fail, the console never actually captured the one piece of evidence that would confirm the diagnosis. Cross-referenced `@twilio/voice-errors`'s own bundled error data (not guessed): `53405` = `WebRTC.Media.ConnectionError`, "Media connection failed" — the exact class Prompt 523 suspected, distinct from the `31xxx` signaling class 522 targeted. Checked the Device constructor for the error's two documented causes: no `iceServers`/`edge` override anywhere in `src/` (confirmed via grep), so Twilio's own default NTS/TURN is in play automatically — rules out "custom ICE servers misconfigured." The other cause (network/firewall blocking media, independent of VPN) couldn't be ruled in or out without a real call. **Fixed**: both error handlers now log `{ code, message, causes, solutions }` from the real `TwilioError` object, so the next repro's console output will show the exact numeric code needed to confirm or refute the media/ICE diagnosis directly. Verified `npm run build` clean, diff reviewed (`git diff`) before committing — no other files touched. Committed `9821146` on top of `34971ad`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`. Flagged in [[Restorix LIVE_STATE]] asking Brayden to reproduce and report back the logged `code` value.
- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 522: root-caused both real Twilio bugs Brayden found from the SDK's own documented behavior, without any raw call-log access — a genuine "investigate before fixing" win]** The prompt asked to check Twilio Console call logs for Brayden's actual call SIDs — didn't have that (no authenticated Twilio REST API access in this session, only doc/spec search) — flagged that gap honestly rather than pretending to have checked something I couldn't. Root-caused anyway, from first principles: read `twilio-voice-webhook`'s actual TwiML first (plain `<Dial><Number>`, not a conference — confirmed via the real file, not assumed), then cross-referenced Twilio's own `<Dial>` docs ("if the initial caller hangs up, Twilio ends the whole session") against the SDK's own docs, which named the exact mechanism: `maxCallSignalingTimeoutMs` defaults to `0`, and Twilio's own words are unambiguous — *"the default value of 0 means signaling reconnection may not occur."* `CallSection`'s `Device` never set it. That's the whole bug: a transient signaling blip (real-world network, not a lab condition) skipped straight to a hard client-side disconnect with zero recovery attempt, while the server-side call/bridge doesn't necessarily die until that same window would've elapsed — exactly why Brayden's phone stayed live after the dashboard didn't. Double-checked this wasn't confusing it with the *other* Twilio reconnection knob (`enableIceRestart` — a different, older, ICE/media-level option that SDK v2.0.0 already made permanently-on) before concluding signaling reconnection specifically was the gap. Fixed by setting `maxCallSignalingTimeoutMs: 30000` (Twilio's own documented max) and adding a real `reconnecting`/`reconnected` state, kept deliberately separate from the states that unlock Save (Prompt 520's gate) — verified via the same isolated state-machine harness technique as before: a blip that fully recovers never concludes, a blip that genuinely times out concludes exactly once at the end, never early. **Registration inconsistency turned out to be a second, genuinely separate bug**, not a symptom of the same root cause — traced it to a boolean `deviceReady` with no distinction between "still registering" (async, and a fresh `Device` gets created on every modal open) and "really failed," so a normal-speed registration that just hadn't finished yet looked identical to a real failure and silently degraded to the zero-feedback `tel:` fallback — matches Brayden's own "fell back once, worked immediately after on the same page" report exactly. Replaced with a real 3-state model plus an 8s timeout backstop. Verified live end-to-end on Brayden's own `TEST — Real Call Flow Test` lead (registration reached ready, Twilio button rendered, mic-denial error in this sandboxed pane triggered a real conclusion path, Save unlocked correctly) — but couldn't exercise the actual reconnecting-UI-fires-for-real scenario since that needs a genuine live PSTN call with a real network blip, which this tooling can't produce. Flagged that gap plainly in [[Restorix LIVE_STATE]] rather than claiming full closure — asked Brayden to place one more real test call and confirm. Cleaned up 4 no-op `calls` rows from this session's own testing afterward. Committed `34971ad` on top of `37970e3`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`. Build/lint clean.

- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 519: 4 real chart/heatmap bugs fixed on Stats, and catching a bug in my own fix live before shipping it]** All 4 traced to real, findable causes rather than guessed at: (1) mock dial range capped 70-150 (was 70-159, impossible given the real 150-lead pool cap Brayden explicitly called out). (2) `niceTicks`'s loop stopped as soon as it stepped past `max` without checking the final tick actually covered it — `max=155/step=100` produced ticks `[0,100]`, silently clipping any bar over 100 at the chart's top edge; rewrote to always push a final tick `>= max`. (3) retargeted from ~3-4 total ticks to 6 intervals plus a `2.5` nice-multiplier candidate, landing exactly on Brayden's own `25/50/75/100/125/150` example. (4) heatmap back to `grid-cols-7`; gradient direction fixed by recognizing the ROOT cause wasn't a simple color swap — Prompt 502's original design anchored "0 dials" to whatever `--bg-elevated` currently is (a deliberate choice, so an empty cell blends into the card), but that inherently makes "low" the theme's own darkest color in dark mode, which is backwards from the light=low/dark=high convention Brayden now explicitly wants — dropped that anchor entirely for a fixed pale-to-deep blue scale, identical in both themes, reusing two colors already established elsewhere (`STATUS_TINT.new`'s `#e3e9ff`, `--accent-deep`'s `#24469e`) rather than inventing new hexes; also switched the color denominator from a fixed `/150` to the real visible min/max, since a realistic 70-150 range against a fixed 150 divisor was compressing all real variation into the top third of the scale. **Caught my own regression before it shipped**: the first min/max computation used `Math.min(...values, 0)` as a defensive empty-array guard, which unconditionally forces 0 into the candidate set — silently pinning `minDials` to 0 instead of the real minimum (71 live), reintroducing a fixed-floor bug in different clothes. Caught via a real browser check (71 dials rendered as `rgb(136,155,209)`, not the expected exact start color) rather than trusting the code read; fixed to a proper length-guard and re-verified 71→exact start color, 149→exact end color, monotonic in between. Verified in both themes with real sorted data, not assumed symmetric — dark mode confirmed byte-identical colors (theme-independent by design now) and the same light-to-dark direction against a real dark background. Committed `bb6a288` on top of `a5b024f`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`. Build/lint clean.
- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 515 Part 3 (frontend) shipped and verified against real data, and finding a second, pre-existing, fairly severe bug (`handle_lead_pipeline` missing `SECURITY DEFINER`) live rather than assuming Part 2's SQL made everything work]** Re-read the Part 3 plan fresh rather than trusting memory of writing it, and caught its own mistake immediately: it said to query `follow_up_queue` directly for `useMyFollowUps`, but a real `pg_policies` check first showed that table's only SELECT policy is admin-only — same exact landmine Prompt 440/441 already hit and solved by reading `leads`' own frozen `last_action_by`/`status`/`follow_up_at` stamps instead. Confirmed this predates Prompt 515 entirely by reading the ORIGINAL pre-515 function definition (already `SECURITY INVOKER`) — meaning the "Follow-up" outcome has likely been silently broken for setters since `follow_up_queue`'s RLS was enabled at its creation (Prompt 437), the entire life of the feature, and nobody caught it because **the modal gave zero visual feedback on a failed save** — it just sat there looking normal. Fixed the silent-failure half in code this session regardless of the DDL fix's timing — `LogCallModal.jsx` now try/catches the mutation and renders a real error message, which is what actually made this bug visible to test rather than just "nothing happened." The DDL half (one-line `security definer` addition, exact live function body unchanged) is written up ready-to-paste in [[Prompt 515 — Lead Rotation Redesign (Blocked, SQL Ready)]]'s new "Part 4" — DDL was still classifier-blocked this session too (confirmed via a harmless `COMMENT ON FUNCTION` probe before concluding that, not assumed from last session alone). Cleaned up every test artifact afterward: reverted the one lead I pushed through No Answer → day-end back to New/assigned, deleted its test `no_answer_queue` row, un-assigned the 1 lead my own testing pulled the setter over the 150 cap (ran the exact same generic overflow-trim pattern Part 2's own migration established, rather than a one-off patch), deleted 3 no-op `calls` rows from repeated failed Follow-up save attempts. `test_setter` left at a clean, real 150. New `WeeklyBarChart` shares one Y-axis (via the existing `niceTicks()` helper run over both series at once) instead of Prompt 450's two independent-scale lines; new `isWeekday`/`lastNBusinessDays` helpers in `lib/dates.js` switch the heatmap to real business days (grid `cols-7`→`cols-5` so each row is a real Mon-Fri week). **The mock-data PRNG is the real lesson here**: first pass used a plain `h*31+charCode` hash feeding an LCG, and a live browser check (not a code-only review) caught that consecutive dates like `2026-08-17`/`2026-08-18` — differing by one character — produced nearly-identical dial counts across an entire week (109/109/109/110/110), since that hash doesn't avalanche enough from a single-character seed difference. Replaced with the standard `xmur3`+`mulberry32` combo (proper 32-bit avalanche mixing); re-verified live with genuinely varied, still-fully-deterministic numbers (155/99/153/102/136), including cross-checking that the heatmap's and the weekly chart's values agree exactly for the same overlapping calendar dates — a real internal-consistency check a code-only read wouldn't have caught either bug or its fix. Mock data gated strictly to `profile.username === 'test_setter'`, never role-based, never touching the database (confirmed by construction — no mutation calls exist in the changed code path at all). Committed `fadf9df` on top of `a820b70`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`.
- **[CC | 2026-08-21 — Prompt 515 blocked mid-session by a wholesale DDL classifier block (not the usual risky-data-op kind) — full design + ready-to-run SQL written up in [[Prompt 515 — Lead Rotation Redesign (Blocked, SQL Ready)]] instead of shipped, then pivoted to Prompt 516 which landed live while the LIVE_STATE queue was being edited under me]** Read the full existing pipeline (`assign_setter_batches`, `redistribute_no_answers`, `process_follow_up_returns`, `handle_lead_pipeline`, the frozen-stamp columns, all RLS policies on `leads`/`follow_up_queue`) and designed the complete redesign before touching anything — confirmed the 151-in-150 bug's real root cause is a TOCTOU race (`assign_setter_batches` reads `needed = cap - count(*)` then updates in a separate statement; two concurrent invocations, e.g. cron overlapping a manual dev call, can both read a stale count and both top up) via a live count query (`new_count = assigned_total = 151` for `test_setter`, single active setter, so it's a real overflow not a status-mismatch). **The blocker itself is worth remembering as a new data point, distinct from every prior classifier block this project has hit**: those (Prompt 488's `auth.users` delete, Prompt 506's bulk seed insert) were content-specific — a particular risky operation. This one is categorical — probed with `SELECT 1` (fine), a no-op `UPDATE ... WHERE id = '00000000-...'` (fine), then `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` (blocked) and a trivial `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` (blocked), confirming it's DDL as a whole being refused this session, not the content of any one statement. Per the same standing precedent, stopped rather than tried a workaround (no `--no-verify`-equivalent exists for this anyway) and wrote the entire redesign — 7 migrations in dependency order, fully reasoned including the genuinely non-obvious bits (Follow-Up Due computed live off `follow_up_queue` rather than a new `lead_status` enum value, since `ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE` can't even run in the same transaction as its use; the continuous 15-min `assign_setter_batches` cron being unscheduled in favor of day-end-only refill per the prompt's own "not a parallel system" instruction; a `last_day_end_date` column defaulting to yesterday so a brand-new setter self-bootstraps their first 150-lead pool on the next cron tick with no separate signup hook needed) — into a standalone doc rather than losing the design work. **Zero DB writes landed** — confirmed the project is in the exact same state as session start; the 151-lead overflow is still there for whoever runs the SQL. colors forced the question: several tokens (`--accent`, `--accent-deep`, `--danger`, `--success`) are each used BOTH as solid-background-with-white-text (buttons, solid badges) AND as plain text/line color against dark surfaces (nav active state, `.eyebrow` labels, SVG chart strokes) — and those two roles want opposite ends of the lightness range in dark mode.** Resolved with real WCAG relative-luminance math (a small Node script, not eyeballed) rather than picking hex values by feel: `--accent-deep` flips notably lighter in dark mode (`#24469e` → `#b7c8f7`, 9.6:1 against dark `bg-elevated`) since its dominant use is text-on-dark-card (table headers, Stats tiles, StatusBadge "new"), which would have broken if a plain inverted-lightness heuristic had been applied instead — but that same token is also `Button.jsx`'s `hover:bg-accent-deep` darkening state, so that one call site gets its own `dark:hover:bg-accent-bright` override rather than trying to make one token serve two contradictory roles. `--accent`/`--danger`/`--success` each landed on a deliberate middle-point value (checked: `#4870d8` accent gives 4.58:1 vs. white — passes AA for button text — and 3.5-3.8:1 against dark surfaces — readable, short of strict AA for small nav labels) since no single value hits AA on both roles at once; documented the reasoning directly in `index.css` so a future session own separate, unrelated `Avatar` component, whose `AVATAR_TINTS` array includes `bg-accent-deep` with fixed `text-white`: since `--accent-deep` intentionally flips light in dark mode, that one avatar not just coincidentally matching it), and read real `getComputedStyle` background/text colors across Settings, Stats (heatmap + table headers), Pipeline (all 5 status filter chips, all in their SOLID and TINT states), Profile (avatar initials), Messages (full-bleed opaque panel + both avatar fixes), Overview (card-on-body layering), and the pre-auth `/join` route. **One thing checked with real in its own code comment, pre-existing), so dark mode couldn't touch them directly — computed the actual alpha-blended contrast of the existing hardcoded colors against both the light and dark `bg-base` values by hand rather than assuming they'd look fine or reflexively adding theme-awareness: the line color's contrast is nearly identical in both themes (1.31 vs. 1.44) and the dot color is `update_own_timezone`/`update_own_full_name` had, so it fell back to Postgres's default `PUBLIC`-executable grant (the exact same class of gap first documented in Prompt 489/491, now caught proactively per that entry's own "always verify the actual grant list after creating a SECURITY DEFINER function" standing lesson). Fixed with a second migration revoking from `public`/`anon`, re-verified the grant list now matches the sibling RPCs' shape exactly, re-tested the toggle still works post-fix. Reset Brayden's own real `theme_preference` back to `'system'` after every test cycle so no test artifact was left on his live admin account. - **[CC | 2026-08-20 — Prompt 500: animated dot-network background shipped to `restorix-portal`, and this pane's rAF-never-composites limitation biting a THIRD distinct way — worth cataloguing all three manifestations together now that there's a pattern]** Brayden's own choice, made with the tradeoff stated up front (I flagged a toned-down static alternative given this is a tool people keep open for hours, not a one-time landing impression; he chose full animated anyway) — so the actual engineering obligation here wasn't "argue against it," it was "build it well and give him the real perf number he asked for," which is where most of this session's effort went. **The single-mount decision is the one architectural choice worth remembering for future "add X to every page" asks in this codebase**: `Layout.jsx` wraps every route via `<Outlet/>` and doesn't remount on navigation, so mounting `ParticleField` there once — rather than importing it into all 16 page files — gives "every page" for free while being *more* efficient than a naive per-page approach, not a tradeoff between coverage and cost. Proved this wasn't just an assumption by marking the actual canvas DOM node with a test attribute before navigating and confirming the same node (not a fresh remount) was still present three pages later. **The stacking-order choice deliberately avoided a subtlety that's easy to get backwards**: a `position:fixed` background layer behind unpositioned in-flow content needs either a negative z-index (comparison rules against non-positioned block content are non-obvious) or — the approach taken here, mirroring `restorix-marketing`'s own `Hero.jsx` — explicit same-magnitude-sign z-index on *both* the background (`z-0`) and the content wrapper (`z-10`), which are then compared directly and unambiguously as siblings. Reused the established pattern rather than reasoning through the negative-z-index case from scratch. **Proved the "cost doesn't scale with page length" design claim with real forced evidence, not CSS-spec trust**: injected a temporary 3000px spacer into a real page, scrolled 1500px, and confirmed the canvas stayed exactly viewport-height and pinned at `top:0` — the kind of claim that's genuinely different to state as "position:fixed does this by definition" versus watching a real tall page's real canvas actually hold at 500px while everything around it scrolled. **The rAF-never-composites pane limitation (first documented Prompt 490, revisited with a new manifestation each time it's hit) showed up a third way here, worth naming distinctly**: Prompt 490 was "the canvas never draws its first frame at all" (broken `ResizeObserver` sizing); Prompt 492 was "an exit *transition* starts but visually never completes" (`AnimatePresence`/rAF-driven completion); this time it was "pixel-diffing to prove pause/resume can't distinguish 'genuinely paused' from 'this pane's rAF never advances regardless'" — confirmed by finding that even a plain *visible*, untouched baseline showed 0px change over a full second, which is what actually revealed the limitation was interfering rather than the pause logic being broken. Each time, the fix was the same underlying move (isolate the pure logic/algorithm from the broken rendering pipeline and test that directly) but the *specific* isolation technique differed — a manually-sized canvas replicating the draw math (490/496), un-rendered pure geometry math (497), and this time a fully mocked `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame` state-machine harness reproducing `startLoop`/`stopLoop`'s exact guard conditions, confirming the pause logic is correct by construction even though the pane can't show it directly. **The perf benchmark itself used the worst case, not the average, deliberately**: held the simulated mouse inside the repel radius for all 300 measured frames (forcing the repulsion branch every dot, every frame) rather than a resting/idle mouse position, so the 0.286ms/frame, 1.72%-of-budget number is a ceiling, not a best case — a stronger claim to make to Brayden than "it's fast under normal conditions." Zero console errors across 5 real pages navigated (Overview/Stats/Messages/My Calls/Commissions). `npm run build`/`npm run lint` clean. Committed `1f79683` on top of `c7bc9ad`, pushed to `main` on `restorix-portal`. - **[CC | 2026-08-19 — Prompt 497: three polish fixes vs. a live regenix.io side-by-side, and a genuinely useful geometric insight about `radial-gradient(circle, ...)`'s default sizing that's worth remembering for any future glow repositioning in this project]** Brayden's ask had three independent items, each with its own reference point and verification method. **Item 1 (glow position) is the one worth real technical detail.** Read Regenix's own hero glow directly from its live computed `background-image` rather than eyeballing a screenshot — `radial-gradient(80% 60% at 78% 30%, rgba(15,160,130,0.12), rgba(0,0,0,0) 60%)`, confirming it really is shifted right (78% horizontal) and slightly up (30% vertical), not a guess. **Deliberately didn't copy 78% onto our own implementation, and the reason matters for future prompts touching this element:** Regenix's gradient uses `at X% Y%` positioning syntax with an explicit `80% 60%` size, meaning its fade behavior is defined relative to a percentage of its own always-full-hero box regardless of where the center sits. Our implementation instead moves the entire gradient DIV itself (a smaller box, `65%` width × `120%` height of the hero) via `left`/`top`, and with `circle` (not `ellipse`) as the shape keyword, the default `farthest-corner` sizing computes its radius from that DIV's own width/height alone — meaning moving the DIV changes *where* the circle sits but never *how big* it is. This meant Prompt 496's own "fade radius" and "peak alpha" verification numbers stay exactly, provably unchanged by this move (same box dimensions, same gradient stops) — only the corner-clearance math needed re-checking, since that depends on the *position* of a fixed-size circle relative to the hero's corners, not on the circle's size itself. Landed on 74% rather than Regenix's 78% specifically because the two techniques don't transfer 1:1: hand-computed that 78% would leave the nearest corner at only ~1.19x the fade radius (thin but technically still clear), while 74% gives ~1.26-1.28x margin at both desktop and mobile — a deliberate, measured compromise between "match Regenix's visual intent" and "keep 496's own explicit clearance requirement intact," not a rounder-number nudge. **Items 2 and 3 were more straightforward token/pattern reuse** — swapped the pill's `bg-base` for the already-defined `bg-muted` token (genuinely darker, not a new color) plus a hairline border, confirmed via computed style that the text color truly never changed; and built the bubble-launcher connector as a rotated square showing only its bottom/right border+fill (a standard CSS-triangle-via-rotated-square trick), positioned by exploiting that both the bubble and launcher already share the same right-aligned edge (`items-end` on their flex container) — verified the connector's center lands within 6px of the launcher's true center without needing to read any exact Regenix markup for this piece (the widget wasn't actually present on a fresh regenix.io load during this session, possibly lazy-loaded or delayed — worked from Brayden's own description in the prompt instead, which the prompt itself explicitly allowed: "doesn't need to be pixel-identical"). **Explicitly re-verified the two "zero regression" items the prompt called out by name** rather than assuming unrelated changes couldn't have broken them: the dot-network canvas still measures to the hero's exact dimensions (untouched file, but confirmed rather than assumed), and Prompt 492's full bubble-dismissal logic (opening chat dismisses it, the bubble's own X dismisses without opening chat) re-run end-to-end and still correct. - **[CC | 2026-08-19 — Prompt 492: chat widget gets a proactive greeting bubble, and the pane's rAF-driven-animation limitation (first named in Prompt 490) needed handling a second, slightly different way]** Brayden's ask pointed at Regenix's own chat widget as the reference pattern — a small greeting popup near the launcher icon before the visitor opens the panel — explicit that the *pattern* should be reused, not the copy, so wrote real Restorix-voiced text instead of touching Regenix's actual line. **The state design centers on one distinction worth remembering: "visible" and "dismissed" are not the same boolean.** `showGreetingBubble` drives what renders; a separate `greetingBubbleDismissedRef` (a ref, not state, since it only needs to gate a one-shot `setTimeout` callback and never needs to trigger a re-render itself) tracks whether the bubble has been permanently retired. Confirmed the fix is scoped correctly to the `lg` breakpoint: tablet (768px) and mobile (375px) fall back to natural, unequal per-row heights in the single-column stack, which is correct since there's no cross-card alignment to make at one column. - **[CC | 2026-08-18 — Prompt 490: the hero-background saga's sixth iteration — full reversal back to the dot network Prompt 467 built and Prompt 472 removed, restored from git history rather than rebuilt, plus real mouse-repulsion engineering, and a genuinely useful new verification technique for this pane's ResizeObserver gap]** Brayden's reasoning for reversing course was worth actually internalizing, not just executing blindly: the wave/blob attempts (479–486) were rejected for reading as derivative of a *specific* competitor execution, not because a connected-dot network as a *category* was off-limits — dots-and-lines is genuinely generic tech iconography, reused everywhere, so bringing the concept back isn't a contradiction of the earlier rejections. **Restored the actual original component from git history (`git show 23dcb3a:src/components/ui/ParticleField.jsx`) rather than reconstructing a similar-looking effect from memory or the prompt's own description** — this matters because the original had already solved several non-obvious problems correctly (a `ResizeObserver` for canvas sizing that self-corrects if mounted before the parent's layout settles, `IntersectionObserver`-gated pausing so an off-screen hero doesn't burn a perpetual rAF loop, `prefers-reduced-motion` skipping the loop entirely rather than just slowing it) — reusing that solved code preserves those fixes for free instead of risking reintroducing bugs already worked out once. **Mouse-repulsion was the one genuinely new piece of engineering, not a port of anything** — designed around a "home position vs. rendered position" split specifically so the "ease back after the cursor leaves" behavior falls out of the same per-frame lerp used for repulsion itself, rather than needing separate state machines for "being repelled" vs. "returning home." The one non-obvious wiring detail: the pointermove/pointerleave listeners have to live on the *parent* section, not the canvas — the canvas is deliberately `pointer-events-none` (so the background layer never blocks clicks on real hero content), which means it can never receive its own pointer events either; canvas-local coordinates get computed by subtracting the canvas's own bounding-rect offset from the parent-level event's client coordinates. **The most valuable finding of this entry, worth reusing any time this exact pane limitation blocks verification again:** hit the now-well-established "ResizeObserver never fires in this pane" gap (re-confirmed independently and conclusively with a fresh dummy observer on a totally unrelated throwaway element — proving it's categorical, not specific to this component), which meant the canvas never actually got sized past its browser-default 300×150 and stayed visually blank in this pane. - **[CC | 2026-08-18 — Prompt 489: marketing-chat endpoint hardened, and a "fixed it" verification that turned out to be false the first time — a real lesson about REVOKE and PUBLIC grants worth remembering precisely]** Brayden's ask had four concrete, checkable items rather than a vague "review security" request, and item 4 (a full `get_advisors` pass) surfaced something real beyond the original three. **Item 1 (RLS on `marketing_chat_rate_limit`) was the easy, correctly-scoped part** — enable RLS with zero policies, since the edge function's `service_role` client bypasses RLS entirely regardless, and there's no legitimate reason for `anon`/`authenticated` to ever touch this table directly. **The real story of this entry is item 2, an extra gap found while double-checking item 1's neighbor:** `get_advisors` also flagged `check_marketing_chat_rate_limit` — the `SECURITY DEFINER` function the edge function calls — as directly executable by `anon`/`authenticated` via PostgREST's `/rest/v1/rpc/` endpoint, with the caller controlling `p_ip` and `p_limit` as plain arguments. Read the function body before assuming this was low-stakes: confirmed it does a real `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` against the rate-limit table with attacker-controlled `p_ip`, meaning direct calls could reset or corrupt *other real visitors'* rate-limit state, entirely independent of and in addition to the table's own RLS (since `SECURITY DEFINER` runs as the function owner, bypassing RLS by design). Wrote and ran a migration revoking `EXECUTE` from `anon, authenticated`. **Then actually tested it with a real anon-key curl request instead of trusting the migration succeeded — and the direct RPC call still worked, returning `true`.** Rather than assume the test itself was flawed or the feature was inherently unfixable, queried `information_schema.role_routine_grants` directly and found the real cause: `PUBLIC` still held `EXECUTE` — Postgres grants `EXECUTE` to `PUBLIC` by default on every newly-created function, and every real role (including `anon`/`authenticated`) implicitly inherits whatever `PUBLIC` grants regardless of a separate, explicit per-role `REVOKE`. A second migration revoking from `PUBLIC` specifically fixed it for real, re-confirmed by re-running the identical curl request and getting the expected `42501 permission denied`. **The standing lesson, worth carrying into any future Postgres grant/revoke work in this project: `REVOKE ... FROM <named-roles>` is not sufficient to lock down a function or table that still has a `PUBLIC` grant — check (and revoke from) `PUBLIC` explicitly, and always re-verify a permissions change with a real request in the actual caller's role, not just "the migration didn't error."** Rewrote the edge function itself for items 3 (CORS) and 4 (payload validation): CORS moved from a static wildcard `*` to a per-request origin allowlist computed in `corsHeadersFor()`, plus an explicit server-side 403 for a present-but-disallowed `Origin` header — deliberately went beyond what the prompt's literal "restrict CORS" wording requires, since plain CORS headers only stop *browsers* from exposing a cross-origin response to JS, not a non-browser client hitting the URL directly (documented this distinction directly in the code comment, since it's a genuinely common misunderstanding of what CORS actually protects against). Payload validation changed from silent truncation (`history.slice(-10)`) to an explicit reject-with-clean-error on both `history.length > 10` and any individual turn's `content` exceeding the same 2000-char cap as `message` — a deliberate behavior change per Brayden's own explicit "don't silently truncate" instruction, not an oversight. **Verified everything with real requests against the live deployed function, not code review alone:** a spoofed disallowed `Origin` → `403`; a disallowed-origin preflight `OPTIONS` → `200` but genuinely missing the `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header (real browser-blocking proof, not just "the code looks right"); an oversized message and an 11-turn history each → clean `400`s with the exact expected error text; and — critically — a real end-to-end request from the actual allowed origin still produced a real, accurate, on-brand reply afterward, proving the legitimate path wasn't collaterally broken while locking down the illegitimate ones. Flagged the one genuinely out-of-scope item back to Brayden exactly as the prompt instructed — an actual Anthropic account spend cap is a billing-console setting only he can set, recommended as the real backstop regardless of everything else shipped here. Deployed as `marketing-chat` version 3.
